- What 0.9.0 reads now, by rule. `deploy-ask`: a cloud CLI that runs a command on a live host or container (`aws ssm send-command`, `aws ssm start-session`, `aws ecs execute-command`, `az vm run-command invoke`, `az container exec`, `az webapp deployment source config-zip`), and every `az` call whose verb is no known read (`az group delete -n rg` asks, `az group list`, a help probe, `az repos pr create` and a call that changes only this machine stay silent). `delete-guard`: a package or a tool version that is removed and a download cache that is emptied, for 25 more package managers (`uv remove requests`, `gem uninstall rake`, `uv cache clean`, `brew cleanup`, `go clean -modcache`). The reader: 30 more tools that run the command after them are read like `timeout` (`unshare -n rm -rf build`, `noglob git push --force origin feat`, `op run -- gh issue close 3`, `hyperfine 'terraform apply -auto-approve'`), and the Homebrew names of the GNU tools are the tools (`grm`, `gcp`, `gtee`, `genv`, `ggrep`). The net: behind a first word no table knows, `git`, `gh` and `az` are still judged, and whatever the rule would answer becomes an ask, because the guard does not know what the word in front does (`mytool git push --force origin feat` asks, `mytool git status` and `echo git commit --no-verify -m x` stay silent). `tracker-guard`: a GraphQL mutation whose name holds `Issue` or `Label` is denied, any other mutation and a document the guard cannot read ask, a query is silent, through `gh api graphql`, `curl`, `wget`, `http`, `https` and `xh`; a merge of a pull request asks (`gh pr merge 3`, `gh api -X PUT repos/o/r/pulls/3/merge`, the same `PUT` through `curl`) and `gh pr merge 3 --admin` is denied. `protected-branch`: `git update-ref` on a protected branch is denied whatever branch is checked out, and `--stdin` asks. A shell that reads its program from a pipe the guard cannot read asks, in every shell rule (`cat run.sh | bash`, `base64 -d x | sh`, `(cat run.sh) | bash`, the same inside `bash -c '…'`); `bash run.sh`, `cat data.txt | bash process.sh` and `yes | bash install.sh` stay silent. `secret-shell`: a command that prints a secret from a store asks (`vault kv get`, `op read`, `op item get`, `kubectl get secret -o yaml`, `gh auth token`, `aws secretsmanager get-secret-value`, `az keyvault secret show`, `doppler secrets get`, `pass`), and the list, status and metadata calls of the same tools stay silent (`kubectl get secret app`). Capitals: the guard's own paths (`.claude/SETTINGS.json`, `tools/GUARD/guard.py`), the GitHub API host (`https://API.GITHUB.COM/…`) and a verb of `brew` or `gem` (`brew UNINSTALL jq`) answer like the plain spelling. The five forms the reviews of group A left: an export inside `eval`, `LEFTHOOK=false`, an option behind the host of `ssh`, a here-document on a group or a subshell, a `case` pattern inside `"$( … )"`.
- Group C, the asks that are gone: `find . -name yarn`; `git restore --staged f`; a search pattern (`grep -rn .env docs`); the previews `git clean -n`, `kubectl delete pod x --dry-run=client`, `git worktree prune --dry-run`; a help probe as the sole argument (`rm --help`); a report that names a secret file and reads nothing of it (`ls -la .env`, `stat .env`, `test -f .env`, `git check-ignore .env`, `git ls-files .env`, `git status .env`, `echo .env >> .gitignore`); `tar --exclude .env`; `command -v codex`; and, inside a client boundary, a search tool's quoted pattern that names another vendor's tool (`grep -n -E 'gemini|openai|ollama' README.md`). The keep list of 2026-11-17 keeps asking (`find . -name .env`, `cat ca.pem`, `chmod 600 .env`, `cp .env.example .env`, `touch .env`, `git reset f`, `git rm --cached f`, `git branch -d x`, `wc -c .env`, `ssh-add`), and so do `ls .env | xargs cat` and `cat $(echo .env)`.
- Decided by the session inside the build, each for the owner to strike. (a) A report is silent only on a line the check reads the way the shell does, and only when the command is that report for certain; on any other line `secret-shell` asks, as 0.8.0 did. The first build looked at the next command only, and a report whose output was handed on another way was silent where 0.8.0 had asked (`{ ls .env; } | xargs cat`, `ls .env 2>&1 | xargs cat`, a line break behind the bar, `f() { ls .env; }; f | xargs cat`, ``cat `ls .env` ``). Since the second round no report is quiet on a line with a here-document, `$_`, a backtick, a bracket in a separator (a subshell, a process substitution, an unquoted `$( )`, a function, a `case` pattern, an array) or a `function` or `coproc`; a separator is read by its characters and anything but `;`, `&&`, `||`, `&` counts as handed on; and every group or compound around the report must close on the line by its own word with such a separator behind it. Since the fifth round the check gives up wherever its reading could differ from the shell's: a quoted or escaped character is no brace, no reserved word and no separator for it (`'{'`, `\}`, `"fi"`); it turns down `$'…'`, `$[…]`, a double-quoted string that ends inside `${`, and a substitution inside a double-quoted string unless it can tell where the shell ends it; a command with `[[` and no `]]`, a word with an open `{` or `[`, a `}` among the words of a command, a closer behind a shell word or with words behind it; and `printf -v` and `[[ … =~ … ]]` are no reports. Since the sixth round `printf` counts only with a first argument that is a format for certain (no option, no `$`, no backtick, and outside quotes nothing a shell expands), a name with a path in front only in `/bin`, `/usr/bin`, `/usr/local/bin` and `/opt/homebrew/bin`, `$_` is found where quotes split it, and a line with a carriage return has no quiet report. Since the seventh round a git report counts only when nothing but `-C <dir>`, `--no-pager` and `-P` stands between `git` and the subcommand and no assignment stands in front of `git`: on a terminal git pipes a report into a pager, and such an option or variable names the program (`git -c core.pager='xargs cat' -p ls-files .env`). Since the eighth round it counts only when nothing else on the line can name that program either: the line holds no `$(`, no backtick and no `${`, quoted or not, and beside the git report stand only other quiet reports that are their words alone, shell words, a closer, and `cd`, `true` and `false`, each without a redirection and without an assignment (`export GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS="'pager.ls-files=xargs cat'"; git ls-files .env`, `git config pager.ls-files 'xargs cat'; git ls-files .env` and `echo "$(git config pager.ls-files 'xargs cat')"; git ls-files .env` ask again); it is a list of what may stand beside a git report, not of what may not; why: this check is the one place in 0.9.0 where a wrong "no pipe here" makes the guard silent on a line that prints a secret file, so it fails closed; what it costs: false asks stay on such lines (`[[ -r .env && -s .env ]]`, `ls .env &`, `time ls -la .env`, `ls -la .env; echo $(date)`, a continuation line in front of `&& echo yes`, zsh's `{ ls -la .env }`, and since the fifth and sixth rounds a report beside `echo "$(basename $(pwd))"`, `echo "$((1 + 2))"`, `echo ${VAR:-not set}`, `echo }` or `echo "${X:-_}"`, `printf "$FMT" .env`, a line with a carriage return, a report tool in a folder of its own such as `./bin/stat .env` or a Nix store path, and since the seventh round a git report with any other option of git or an assignment in front, such as `git -c color.ui=always status .env` or `LC_ALL=C git status .env`, and since the eighth round a git report beside any command that is none of those named, or on a line with a substitution, such as `git add -A && git status .env`, `mkdir -p tmp; git status .env` or `echo "$(date)"; git status .env`); what would change it: these asks showing up in the replay, which found none in 45 days. (b) A pipe the guard cannot read is a finding of the read and not its end. The build raised at such a pipe, which ended the read of the whole line, so `bash -c 'git push --force origin feat'; cat run.sh | sh` went from a deny at 0.8.0 to an ask: one pipe added to a line turned a refusal into a prompt. Now the reader notes the pipe and reads on, a deny found anywhere on the line wins, and otherwise the ask for the pipe joins the other asks. (c) A pipe through which the guard has read the script is no unread pipe: `echo 'git commit --no-verify -m x' | { sh; }` is denied as at 0.8.0, and the decision is taken from what the reader read, not from the look of the command behind the bar (a first try by the look made `cat run.sh | { ssh -n build; sh; }` silent for six rules). (d) An option of a search tool that the guard does not know means the pattern of that call is not known: no word is left out of the secret check and no pattern is protected inside a client boundary, as at 0.8.0. The build had read every unknown option as one that takes the next word, so `rg --mmap TOKEN .env` took TOKEN for the option's value and `.env` for the pattern, and was silent (48 of the 248 flags of rg 15, GNU grep 3.12 and `git grep` 2.55 did that). (e) The dry-run options of `git clean` and `git worktree prune` are read as git reads them: the last of `-n`, `--dry-run`, `--no-dry-run` and their prefixes wins, and an option the guard cannot place asks (`git clean --excl -n -f` removes files: `--excl` takes `-n` as its pattern; measured with git 2.55 in a scratch repository). (f) HTTPie: `--raw` is a body; an `http`, `https` or `xh` call to the GraphQL address that shows no document asks, because in an agent's shell the standard input is never a terminal and HTTPie sends it; a document on the line decides, since HTTPie refuses a request item beside a body on standard input (measured with HTTPie 3.2.4 and `--offline`, which builds the request and sends nothing), and that makes `http POST https://api.github.com/graphql query='query { viewer { login } }' < q.json` silent where the second fix round had asked; `wget --body-data` and `--body-file` read as `--post-data` and `--post-file` (Wget 1.25.0, from its help). (g) The files a line writes by redirection are read on the line as the shell joins it, for `secret-shell` only (`echo x > \` and `.env` on the next line). (h) Wider than the rows, on purpose: every `op item get` asks; `git update-ref` compares the ref name without case on every file system (on the owner's, `refs/heads/Main` is the ref of `main`; elsewhere this denies a write to another branch); a pipe with a subshell, a group or a compound on one side and a shell on the other asks even where the guard could read the script in the plain form. (i) The hook switch is read behind `command -p`, `builtin --`, a shell word (`{`, `then`, `do`, `!`) and `time`, as the condition of a compound, and in a group, a compound or a function body that closes before the commit (measured with bash 5.3, zsh 5.9 and `sh`: the variable reaches the commit in each form); cost: none; compliance: (b) and (c) keep a deny a deny; what would change any of them: his word, or a measured case where the reading is wrong.
- The rows, and what grew after `owner ready`. Frozen with #57: 968 shell rows and 36 file rows in six files, their hashes in the item. The acceptance set then grew eleven times, each time from findings and never to relax a frozen row: 371 rows in the first fix pass (267 from the review, 104 from five addenda the session wrote while the pass ran), 188 in the second fix round, 146 in its addendum, 40 with the review fixes, 48 in the fourth round, 151 in the fifth, 97 in the sixth, 59 in the seventh, 89 in the eighth, 25 after its review and 8 after the planted breaks of that head. Those 33 were due at no commit: they hold at 0.8.0 as well and pin outcomes that had no row (bullets on how it was reviewed and on what the session's checks found). At the head 2,190 shell rows and 36 file rows hold; each row is a case of `tools/guard/tests/test_table_forms.py`, in 22 tables, and the session compared each table line for line with its file. The rows of the fourth to eighth rounds were each run in zsh 5.9.1 and bash 5.3.15 against a stand-in file before they were frozen (those of the seventh and the eighth under a pseudo-terminal, in a scratch repository, with git 2.55.0): a row that expects an ask for a hand-on prints the file in at least one of the two, and no silent row prints it. Three notes on the frozen set. One unseen form of the drafting session is wrong and stays red: `echo brew UNINSTALL jq` was expected silent, but `echo brew uninstall jq` asks at 0.8.0 already (a table tool named as a word of `echo` text asks), and the capital spelling now follows its plain twin. Criterion 14 ("nothing that asked or denied at 0.8.0 stops doing so, apart from the rows of criterion 13") is narrower than the item's own rows: the rows of criterion 3 ask for `ggrep -rn .env docs` to be a search, and those of criterion 12 take a false deny away. And four pinned rows of group A changed with criterion 7, which asks for exactly that (`cat script.sh | bash` and three kin: silent at 0.8.0, an ask now).
- How it was built: test first, against the rows, one commit per step, by Opus engineers (`python-engineer`), each with a brief of its own; the orchestrating session wrote the briefs and the rows, built nothing in the guard but the version line, and checked every commit before the next was ordered. Engineer 1 built the 17 steps on 2026-11-18 (it stopped once after step 13, as its brief told it to when its context ran short, and was resumed) and made the first fix pass (22 commits). Its context was full after that, so a second engineer made the second fix round (7 commits), a third its addendum (3 commits) and a fourth the review fixes (4 commits) and, resumed three times, the fourth, fifth and sixth rounds (one commit each); a fifth built the seventh round and, resumed three times, the eighth and the two sets of rows added after it (one commit each). The design of those five rounds was written and tried by the session on a scratch copy of the guard and handed over as a diff with its rows. Six agents in all with the reviewer: five is the limit the owner set, and the sixth was started on his answer of 2026-11-19 to the session's question who builds the seventh round ("Sixth agent"); a finished agent that is resumed is no new one; cost: none per month. The build itself, counted by this repository's ledger from the transcripts at the time of writing: about 305 USD of API-equivalent use on the owner's subscription, 255 USD of it the two orchestrating sessions (the larger model, with a long context read again at every step) and 49 USD the six agents (Claude Code's own figure for the first session is 281 USD where the ledger counts 252 from its transcript; the sums here take the ledger's count for both sessions), plus 5.55 USD for the five runs of `bin/fresh-review` named in the next bullet; why named: the orchestrating sessions cost several times what the agents did, which is the wrong way round for a build of this kind, and the lesson is in the last bullet.
- How it was reviewed. One separate Opus agent with the reviewer's role read the build head `8879cd5` and fixed nothing: BLOCKER, 22 findings (8 blocker, 8 major, 6 minor), each with a probe file. All eight blockers were weaker than 0.8.0 or silent where a criterion asks for a verdict: a report in a group whose output is piped on; a line the reader cannot split; `git clean -n --no-dry-run`; `git restore --staged --work f`; the value of `--include` or `-g` read as a pattern; a search tool behind `ssh`; and two ways to send a GraphQL mutation unseen. The first fix pass closed 19; three were left by decision and are named in the bullet on what the change does not cover (Azure read verbs that ask now, `go clean -modcache -h`, U+017F in the guard's own paths). `bin/fresh-review` (a diff, through the egress screen, to one Opus context without tools) ran five times before this block was written. A sixth run reads the block itself, with the rows added after the fifth, the docs and the evidence folder; a record cannot hold the review of itself, so that result stands in the pull request. On the build (`769b9d2..8879cd5`): COMMENT, 6 findings, 1.14 USD; three spellings went into the fix pass (`--work`, `--no-dry-run`, the case of a ref name), one into its fifth addendum (`go clean -modcache=true`), and two were about the record (no line here; which mutations are denied), which this block answers. On the first fix pass (`8879cd5..d1052b1`): COMMENT, 5 findings, 0.69 USD; three went into the second round (a `case` pattern's bracket in the check for a piped shell, `command -p export`, a port behind the API host), the fourth was a wall-clock bound in a test, and the fifth was that three widenings had no line here ((h) above). On the second round and its addendum (`d1052b1..abab75a`): COMMENT, 5 findings, 1.30 USD: the list that marks an unread pipe was optional in two functions, and without it the check was skipped; no caller left it out, but a later one could, so it is required now. The count that had replaced the wall-clock bound pins one cause of slowness only, so the test has both now, with the hook's own budget of 3.5 s as the bound (the line takes 0.012 s). `secret-shell` compared a message text to learn that the read went through; it gets a mark from the reader pass instead. And two changes of behaviour had no line here ((b) and (f) above). On the review fixes and the fourth to seventh rounds (`abab75a..2a28378`, read from a checkout of that commit, so that the text the merge of `main` brought in was not read again): COMMENT, 3 findings, 1.41 USD. One MAJOR: a pager that another command of the same line sets left a git report quiet (`export GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS="'pager.ls-files=xargs cat'"; git ls-files .env`). The session had named that as a limit; the review called it the attack of the seventh round one command apart, and it became the eighth round. Two MINOR ones are answered in this block: `/usr/local/bin` and `/opt/homebrew/bin` count as system folders although the user can write to them (kept and named in the bullet on what the change does not cover; a bare `ls` found through `PATH` has the same exposure), and the asks the strict reading adds had no line in that diff ((a) above). On the eighth round (`bb28137..b2f4e2a`, the version line with it): COMMENT, 3 findings, 1.01 USD. One MAJOR: the list of words that run a command while the shell expands them names `$(`, a backtick and `${`, and not the forms that open with a bare bracket (a process substitution `<( )` or `>( )`, zsh's `=( )`, a glob qualifier such as `*(e:'…':)`); the diff did not show whether such a line beside a git report asks, and no row said so. It asks: the session measured 28 such lines under a pseudo-terminal, a shell prints the file on 18 of them, and the guard asks on every one, at 0.8.0, at the head of the seventh round and at the head of the eighth, because the lexer ends a command at the bracket: a line with a bracket in a separator has no quiet report (second round), and what stands inside the brackets is then a command of the line that is not on the eighth round's list of what may stand beside a git report. Either rule alone keeps all 28 asking; with both taken out of a scratch copy, 23 still ask. The engineer of the eighth round had named the same doubt in his report. What was missing was the row and a word at the list: 25 rows and a comment were added (`8f0a893`, no line of code changed: the syntax tree of the guard without its docstrings is the same before and after). Two MINOR ones: a `cd` into another repository puts a git report under the configuration of that repository, which the docstring of the rule did not name (it does now; the limit is in the bullet on what the change does not cover), and the asks the round adds had no line in that diff ((a) above); compliance: the reviewer's report with its probe files, the receipts and every engineer's report are in `evidence/2026-11-19-guard-0.9.0-groups-b-c/`.
- What the session's own checks found beyond the reviews, and why there were eight fix rounds where the handover named one. After the first fix pass the session judged every command of every row again in rewritings that keep what the command does (a line break behind a bar, `{ CMD; }`, `( CMD )`, `L | (R)`, `L 2>&1 | R` and fourteen more): 918 cells in 66 commands were weaker than the command as written, 831 of them an ask that became silent and 87 a deny that became silent, nearly all in the two checks the build had added (the quiet report and the pipe into a shell); neither review had seen them, and the rows could not, because each row is one spelling. That is the second round ((a) above). A second check then laid the new guard beside 0.8.0 on the rewritten text and with a second command on the same line: 1,991 cells in 159 commands went from a deny to an ask ((b) above), and 41 forms of a script fed through a group ((c)). The planted breaks found the third: of 54 one-line breaks in the code of the second round and the addendum, 12 let every test pass; ten of them are pinned by the 40 rows of the review fixes and two tests (among them an unread pipe inside a nested script, `bash -c 'cat run.sh | sh'`, which no row had), and two were taken to change no verdict on any line that bash or zsh accepts (57,270 generated lines, checked with `bash -n` and `zsh -n`). For one of the two that was wrong, as the run of every list at the head of the seventh round showed: without the rule that turns down a line with `coproc`, a report redirected into the descriptor of a coprocess is silent, and bash prints the file (`coproc xargs cat >&2; ls .env >&${COPROC[1]}; exec {COPROC[1]}>&-; wait`). The guard asked there all along; what was missing was the row, and the generated lines had no such redirection. The same run showed a second line without a row: the default that takes a construct which never closes for handed on, whose rows the fifth round had made idle. Five rows of the eighth round pin both. The fourth round came from the engineer of the review fixes, whose rewriting check showed nine cells silent on lines that zsh runs: zsh closes a group at a `}` that is no first word (`{ { ls .env; true } | xargs cat; }`), which bash refuses. The fifth came from one cell. With the fourth round in, the check beside 0.8.0 showed one cell weaker that no row asked for: `time { true; { ls .env; true } | xargs cat; }`, silent where zsh prints the file. It was the tip of a class as old as the second round: the quiet check was silent whenever its reading of a line's structure differed from the shell's in the right way, and it differed in six: a brace among the words of a command, or an opener behind a word the check does not pass over (`time {`, `repeat 1 {`, `always {`); a closer behind a shell word, which zsh takes (`else fi`), or behind a closer, which bash takes (`fi fi`, `} }`); a quoted or escaped brace, reserved word or separator (`'{'`, `\}`, `echo ';' '{'`); the lexer splitting where the shell does not (`[[ a && { ]]`, `${X:-a; { b}`, `a[x ; { ]=1`); quotes the lexer reads otherwise than the shell (`$'\''`, `"${X:-" … "}"`, a quote inside a substitution of a double-quoted string); and a report that hands its words on by itself (`printf -v x %s .env; cat "$x"`, `[[ .env =~ .* ]]; cat $BASH_REMATCH`, `ls .env; cat "${_:-x}"`). The session measured each form with zsh 5.9.1 and bash 5.3.15 against a stand-in file, then wrote a check that builds lines from parts (a report, 26 constructs around it, words a reader could take for an opener or a closer, 8 constructs around the whole), judges each with the guard and runs the silent ones in both shells. In a sample of 150,019 of them the head of the fourth round was silent on 4,460 lines that a shell prints, and 0.8.0 on none. A random generator written one round earlier had found nothing in 200,000 lines: random text does not reach these combinations, parts that are put together do. The sixth round came from the engineer's note on the fifth: `printf '%s\n' -v .env` asked, because any word that starts with `-v` counted as the option. Asking where the option can stand showed the opposite gap, an option the shell makes: `X=-v; printf $X x %s .env; cat $x` and `printf {-v,x} %s .env; cat $x` were silent, and zsh and bash print the file. The session then went through what else makes a report no report, and through the single characters the lexer and the shell read differently (a check of its own: every character from U+0001 to U+007F glued to a brace, a separator or the report in 25 line shapes, 3,300 lines). Four more kinds came out: `$_` that quotes split in the text and `eval` puts together (`ls .env; eval 'cat $'_`, bash); a carriage return, a blank for the lexer and a plain character for the shell, so `}` with one glued on closed a group for the check only (bash); a report's name with a path in front, which is another program (`script/test .env`, `ln -s /bin/cat ./ls; ./ls .env`); and `case` in a quoted substitution with no blank in front (`"$(true;case x in x) … esac)"`), which the fifth round had looked for between blanks. With the parts of both rounds the enumerating check has 4,644,681 lines: the head of the fifth round is silent on 13,420 of them, 6,822 of which a shell prints; the head is silent on 3,456 and no shell prints one. 151 and 97 rows pin the two rounds; of 63 planted breaks, one per rule and sub-rule of both, every one turns a test red; and a fuzz lays the check's reading of quotes beside the lexer's on 400,000 random strings and finds no difference. The seventh round came from the engineer's note on the sixth: git hands a report's output to a pager, and the pager is a program the command itself can name (`git -c core.pager='xargs cat' -p ls-files .env`, `git -c pager.ls-files='xargs cat' ls-files .env`, `GIT_PAGER='xargs cat' git -p ls-files .env`). The session's first measurement showed nothing: git starts a pager only when its output is a terminal, and the shells of the measurement had none. Under a pseudo-terminal zsh and bash print the file on each of the 31 lines the round is about, where 0.8.0 asks and the head of the sixth round was silent. The shell tool of Claude Code gives a command no terminal; the terminal of an editor does, and the kit is used there too. 59 rows pin the round; of 20 planted breaks 19 turn a row red, and the twentieth changes a verdict only where a shell word stands between an assignment and `git`, seven lines on which neither shell runs git. The eighth round came from the same engineer's note on the seventh and from the review by a fresh context: the pager can be named by another command of the same line, through the environment (`export`, `declare -x`, `typeset -x`, `set -a`, `eval`), through git's configuration (`git config pager.ls-files 'xargs cat'`, a redirection into `.git/config`), or by a word that runs such a command while the shell expands it (a quoted `$( )`, a backtick, `${ …; }` in bash 5.3, a quoted subscript that `[[ -v … ]]` evaluates). The session had put that down as a limit, beside the pager an earlier call leaves in the configuration; the review did not accept the line between the two, and it was right: the rows of the seventh round pinned the same attack with the variable one word further left. Under a pseudo-terminal a shell prints the file on each of the 46 lines the round is about, where 0.8.0 asks and the head of the seventh round was silent. 89 rows pin the round, and of 25 planted breaks 25 turn a row red. The 25 rows added after its review pin the forms that open with a bracket; two rules each hold all of them, so no single planted break turns one of them red (with the bracket rule and the rule of the eighth round both taken out, five do). The same effect, the other way round, showed in the run of every list of planted breaks at that head (`8f0a893`): two breaks of the seventh round that had turned a row red at its own head let every test pass now (`--paginate` put on the list of plain git options, and `--config-env` with its value taken like `-C`). The seventh round's rows for those two each carry a second command or an assignment (`export X='xargs cat'; git --config-env pager.ls-files=X ls-files .env`), and since the eighth round that alone makes the line ask, so the rows no longer tested the rule they were written for. The guard asked on the bare forms all along (`git --paginate ls-files .env`); eight rows on which nothing but the seventh round's rule decides pin them (`6b83c85`, tests only); why recorded: a release of the guard is now checked in rewritings, beside the old guard on the rewritten text, with a second command on the line, and, where a check goes silent on shell grammar, with lines built from parts and run in the real shells, before it is called done; the scripts that do it are kept (last bullet).
- What the checks showed at `6b83c85`, the last commit that touches the guard or its tests (2026-11-19, the owner's machine; the last one that changes a rule is `b2f4e2a`, the eighth round; `8f0a893` after it changes comments and adds rows, and the guard of the two is the same program; `6b83c85` adds rows only, so its guard file is that of `8f0a893` byte for byte, and the checks that read the guard file alone, which are the runs under a terminal, the lines built from parts, the per-character check and the fuzz, ran at `8f0a893`; the commits behind `6b83c85` are the docs, this record and the evidence, and lint and the suite ran again on them, with the same counts): `ruff check tools` clean; 7,541 passed, 9 skipped on Python 3.13 and on Python 3.9 (5,271 passed at 0.8.0); 2,190 shell rows and 36 file rows hold; forms the engineers never saw, in 8 lists of 949 rows: every due row holds, and every row that must keep its verdict does, but the wrong one named above; over 6,083 commands in three contexts (a feature branch, `main`, a client boundary; 145,992 verdicts) 1,343 are weaker than 0.8.0: 282 that a due row asks for, and 1,061 in 377 commands that no due row asks for, every one read by a session against its kind: a kind group C takes away (a report, a preview, a help probe, a search pattern, the value of an exclude option) or a limit named below (an alias in front of a report, a name that goes through a file); 248 of 248 unknown flags of the search tools ask in front of `TOKEN .env`; the rewriting check over the row files: 35 cells in 2 commands turn from a deny to an ask, and none ends silent, each a script the guard reads in the plain form that goes through a subshell or out of a group, where the line asks (stronger than 0.8.0, which is silent there for `git-guard`); the same check beside 0.8.0: no cell in which the new guard is weaker than the old on the rewritten text, apart from four kinds read one by one (a report behind a continuation line, a harmless command in a quoted substitution, a preview behind a continuation, and a row with a carriage return whose rewriting drops it); each code path of the fix rounds broken once in a scratch copy: 172 breaks in five lists, 166 of which still apply to the code (six of the second round's list change lines that later rounds replaced, and the lists of those rounds break the new lines), and all 166 turn a test red; the check for removed tests passes without a label, and the branch touches no workflow.
- The replay, the hook timing, and what the merge ask means in daily work. The recorded tool calls of 45 days on the owner's machine (594 transcripts from 72 project folders, 22,167 calls on 15 days, the first on 2026-10-14) were handed to guard 0.8.0 and to the head, nothing run: no deny is lost (167 at both, counted over every rule), 2 asks of `secret-shell` are gone (a search pattern and a line of `echo` text that name a template file), and 27 asks are new, all `tracker-guard` on `gh pr merge`; inside a client boundary 16 asks of `egress-guard` go as well (30 at 0.8.0, 14 at the head: a search tool's quoted pattern that names another vendor's tool, and `command -v`). `secret-shell` asked 22 times in those calls at 0.8.0 and asks 20 times at the head: the kinds of group C were rare in the recorded work, and the gain of this release is in what it reads, not in prompts saved. The fifth, the sixth, the seventh and the eighth round change no verdict of those calls, each against the head before it. No `az` call is in those 45 days, so what the `az` rule costs shows only where `az` is in use. One hook call through the real entry point, median of five, 0.8.0 → 0.9.0: a plain command 34.0 → 40.3 ms, the longest acceptance row 33.8 → 41.2 ms, a line of 100 kB 317 → 325 ms, and 100 kB in capitals 633 → 696 ms, against a budget of 3.5 s. The consequence the owner chose with answer (6): from 0.9.0 on, every `gh pr merge` a session runs on his machine asks him first, 27 times in the last 45 days, and his tap is the record of that merge; a session that merges under the standing word of 2026-11-18 still needs that tap. In a Copilot cloud agent, which treats an ask as a deny, an agent can no longer merge, run an `az` write, print a stored secret or pipe an unread script into a shell; cost: none; revisit if: the taps on merges become a reflex (then the deny, with the merge left to the owner or to the check of plan step 2.2).
- Where this change left the usual path. (a) Eight fix rounds and five engineers where the handover named one engineer and one fix pass; why: bullet above; the fourth engineer was resumed for three rounds, and the fifth, the sixth agent of the build and one over the limit of five, was started on the owner's answer of 2026-11-19 and resumed three times, for the eighth round and for the two sets of rows added after it, without a new question to him. (b) Six commits of the second round are red: its engineer put the whole row table into the commit of topic 1, so `4a23f30` to `87b53f4` carry rows that only the later topics turn green; the head of the round is green, the history is kept as it is (no force push), and `git bisect` over those six needs the row test skipped. (c) Engineers broke a rule of their brief three times and said so in their reports: one shell call with `cd`, one empty here-document, three topics in one commit. (d) The review by a fresh context ran in parts, because the whole diff is over the 200,000 characters the egress screen lets through; each part was read against the head before it. After the merge of `main` into the branch the range from `abab75a` held about 50,000 characters that `main` had brought in and that were reviewed with their own pull requests, so the code of the review fixes and of the fourth to seventh rounds was read from a second checkout at `2a28378`, a scratch worktree of the session; the eighth round was read after it against the merge commit, and the two commits of rows after that review, the docs and this record against the head of the eighth round. The text `main` brought in was not read again, and the receipt of the last run cannot stand in the folder that run read: the pull request names it. (e) The session merges this pull request itself, under the owner's standing word of 2026-11-18 (block of that day): the merger is the Claude Code session `f4bb0a13-ab54-4963-986b-94e0bba5874e` on his machine (model Claude Fable 5.2), with the owner's token, once the checks of the pull request's head are green and the review by a fresh context has left no open finding; the pull request's checklist names the head, the checks and the reviews, and says how each finding was closed. The branch touches no workflow and needs no `test-removal-ok` label, so neither exception of that word applies. (f) The item stays in `status/review` after the merge, by the owner's sentence of 2026-11-18 ("next time I do owner done xx it has to be after whole phase"); no session runs an owner verb. (g) The orchestrating session was cut once, on 2026-11-19 after the sixth round, at the owner's suggestion: its context had grown to 366,000 tokens. The second session took the state from a note the first had written, measured the open question of the pager, and ran every check of the head itself.
- What it does not cover (measured at the head; the guard reads text and prompts, it is no sandbox). False asks that stay: Azure verbs that only read and are in no list (`az group export`, `az storage blob url`, `az acr task logs`, `az policy state summarize`, `az network watcher test-connectivity`: tell the owner and the verb goes into `_AZ_READ_VERBS`); `go clean -modcache -h`; a GraphQL query that only holds the word (`search(query: "mutation testing")`); a query whose value starts with `$` (`-f query="$Q"`); `http --ignore-stdin`, `http --offline` and `http GET` to the GraphQL address, which send no document; the bar of a `case` pattern, which is read as a pipe, so `case "$SHELL" in */bash|*/zsh) echo ok;; esac` asks in every rule; `aws ecr get-login-password | docker login --password-stdin …`, the everyday form of a store call, which asks by the engineer's reading of "prints a stored secret" (kept, with `aws sts get-session-token`, `gcloud auth application-default print-access-token` and `az … list-publishing-profiles`); a table tool named in the text of an `echo` (`echo gem uninstall rake`); a quoted separator in front of a command name, which the lexer takes for a separator (`echo ';' git push --force origin feat` is denied; older than this build); and the lines of decision (a). Asks where a deny would be right: a script the guard reads in the plain form (`echo 'git push --force origin feat' | sh`: deny) that goes into the shell through a subshell or a compound, behind another command of a group, or out of a group (`… | (sh)`, `{ echo '…'; } | sh`); a GraphQL mutation that comments on or assigns an issue (`addComment`: the deny is for a name that holds `Issue` or `Label`). Denies that are too wide: a function body that sets the hook switch and is never called (`f() { export HUSKY=0; }; git commit -m x`). The limits of the quiet reports, where 0.8.0 asked because it asked on every report, and each needs a line written to get round the guard: a name that goes through a file (`echo .env > list.txt; xargs cat < list.txt`, which is inherent in `echo .env >> .gitignore` being quiet); a line that changes what a report's name runs (`alias`, `hash`, `PATH=`, `zmodload zsh/stat`, `enable -f`: the check takes a name for what it says, and it takes `/usr/local/bin` and `/opt/homebrew/bin` for system folders although the user can write to them); a pager or another program that git takes from its configuration or from the environment as an earlier call or the session left them, where git's output is a terminal (a `git config pager.ls-files …` of an earlier call, a variable exported before the line, the configuration of the folder that `-C` or `cd` names: since the eighth round nothing on the line itself can set it, and the guard reads a line); and what a line computes at run time or reads from its own text (`x=_; ls .env; cat ${!x}` in bash, a name reference to `_`, a `DEBUG` trap with `$BASH_COMMAND`). Where the quiet check stops, by its kind: a report beside a read the guard never saw (`ls .env; cat .e*`) is silent where 0.8.0 asked, because 0.8.0 asked on the report and no version sees that read; no rule on reports can close it, and the read is in the next bullet. Silent, and named so that nobody reads the guard as covering it: an interpreter fed by a pipe (`cat run.py | python3`), and `| source /dev/stdin`, `| rbash`, `xargs -I{} sh -c '{}'`; a shell behind a remote tool (`cat run.sh | ssh build bash`); a script given by a substitution, a variable or a file (`bash -c "$(cat run.sh)"`, `echo "$X" | bash`, `bash run.sh`); a name or a script carried through a variable (`f=.env; cat "$f"`, `ls ${x:=.env}; cat $x`, `echo cat .env > run.sh; sh run.sh`); a shell fed inside a `case` (`cat run.sh | case x in a) bash;; esac`); the REST call that merges a branch (`repos/o/r/merges`) and the approval of a review (`gh pr review 3 --approve`); `op document get`, `sops exec-env`, `ansible-vault edit`; `LEFTHOOK=off` and `LEFTHOOK=FALSE` (`false` and `0` are read); the hook switch behind zsh's `noglob`, through `setopt allexport`, or set by `read` or `printf -v`; `aws` and `gcloud` writes, `printenv`, a bare `env`, by the item's scope limit. Not measured: `xh` is read like HTTPie from its help and is not installed on this machine. Left by the review: the guard's own paths written with U+017F (`.claude/ſettings.json`) pass `guard-config`, since `str.lower()` does not fold that letter and the file system may.
- Gaps older than this change, found while it was checked, silent at 0.8.0 as well, and filed as one backlog item (#71) because each needs a decision of its own: the value of an include or glob option glued to it (`grep -r --include=.env TOKEN .`, `rg --glob=.env TOKEN`: it reopens the 0.8.0 decision on pattern options); a secret file reached by a glob or a zsh form (`cat .env*`, `cat .en?`, `for f (.env) cat $f`, and `{ cat .env}`, where zsh takes the brace off the name); a secret name with a substitution or an expansion glued to it (`cat ".env$(true)"`, `cat .env"$(true)"`, `cat ".env${X}"`: silent in 0.7.1 and 0.8.0 as well, both shells print the file; the form with a backtick asks); `echo x >| .env`; writes to `~/.ssh/config` and `~/.ssh/authorized_keys`; the deny for an issue write with a port in the address (`https://api.github.com:443/repos/o/r/issues/3`), and HTTPie to that path with its body in `--raw`; a script an `echo` feeds to a remote shell through a subshell or a group (`echo 'rm -rf build' | (ssh build)`); a command in a git configuration value or a pager variable of a git call that is no report (`GIT_PAGER='…' git log`), and a secret path inside such a value or as the value of an assignment (`git -c core.pager='cat .env' -p status`, measured under a pseudo-terminal: silent in both versions, both shells print); a quoted word that a test of bash evaluates (`[[ -v 'a[$(cat .env >&2)]' ]]`, the same with `test -v` and with `-eq` in `[[ ]]`: bash 5.3 runs the substitution in the subscript and prints the file, zsh does not); a continuation line in the first pass of every rule but `secret-shell` (`find . -name '*.tmp' \` and `-delete` on the next line); a fed script that writes a secret file by redirection; `curl -K req.cfg` and `curl -F query=@q.graphql`.
- Kept for the next change to the guard, and the session's lessons. The scripts that judge a guard in rewritings, beside the old guard and with a second command on the line, the check that builds lines from parts and runs the silent ones in zsh and bash, the per-character check, the script that runs the rows of a round in both shells, the planted-break runner with its lists, the row files and the briefs are scratch files of the owner's machine and are not on `main`: a reader of `main` alone cannot run those checks again, only read their output in the evidence folder. They are kept on the branch `docs/handover-2026-11-17` beside the tools of group A, in `docs/handover-2026-11-17/g090/` as of commit `d93bf4c` (271 files, the paths of the machine replaced, nothing the replay wrote). Bringing them onto `main` as tools with tests of their own is deferred and is the owner's to order; the review by a fresh context asked for this sentence. On cost: the orchestrating session ran as one long context from the first brief to this block, and every step read that context again; the next build of this size is cut into sessions at each hand-over to an engineer, with the state in files, which is what the handover branch already holds. On method: a check that goes silent on shell grammar is tested with lines built from parts and run in the real shells, not with random text and not with rows alone; where a second reading of a line is added to a first, the two are laid side by side on random strings before the build goes on; of every command on a quiet list one asks whether it is that command for certain: its options, its name, the program behind the name; a measurement of what a command prints gives the command what it has in use, a terminal included (the pager of the seventh round printed nothing through a pipe); a planted break that no test catches is run in the shells before it is called harmless (the `coproc` rule was not); and a limit the session draws for its own design is a claim like any other: the review by a fresh context moved this one, where the session had read its own stopping rule into the code; and a row that a later rule holds as well stops testing the earlier rule, so every list of planted breaks is run again at each new head, not only the list of the round.

## 2026-11-18 — the agent view: `ledger.py agents` is a tree in the terminal, read from local files (#59)

- A terminal tree and no dashboard. `tools/telemetry/ledger.py agents [--session ID] [--watch]` prints one session: the session on the first line, under it every agent it started (the agents of a workflow under the workflow, an agent under the agent its meta file names), per line the state, the duration, the cost, the tool calls and the model, then a total and a few lines that say what the columns mean. It is one verb in a file the installer already ships (`kit.manifest`), standard library only: no server, no port, no page in a browser, no second store. why: the question ("what is running, and what did each agent cost") is asked at the terminal the session runs in, and the data lie in local files the ledger already reads; the dashboard that was read for this (`hoangsonww/Claude-Code-Agent-Monitor`, commit `a46ad70f2715`) needs Node.js 22.22 or newer, `npm run setup`, an Express and WebSocket server, SQLite and a React page on ports 5173 and 4820, which is an install on the developer machine (the rule of 2026-11-16 forbids it) and a second copy of session data outside the telemetry folder; cost: $0 a month, no process, no dependency; compliance: the view writes nothing and sends nothing, so it adds no record to keep or to protect, and it prints no prompt, no tool input and no tool output. What it does print from a session is the type and the short description the session gave each agent: the tree of a client session stays where that session's data may be (README); what would change it: several people who must watch the same sessions, or a view over many sessions at once.
- Projects read, their licences, what was taken (criterion 9 of the item). Read on 2026-11-18 as source text, fetched through the GitHub API into a scratch folder outside the repository; nothing was run and no code was copied. `niemes/cc-agent-tree` (MIT, (c) 2026 Niemes, commit `4626ae5c46f7`; Python, an interactive TUI for a true-colour terminal of at least 84 x 40 cells): taken as ideas are that no parent edge is invented (here: a parent only where a meta file names one), that an unfinished tool call is no proof of a live process (here: the state is called inferred and the limit is printed), the replacement of non-printable characters before printing, the alternate screen, and a frame that is written only when it changed; its `ui.py` also counts a wide character as two columns, which reached the view through the review and is the same call of the standard library; the interactive TUI was not taken. `zemixo/claude-agent-ledger` (MIT, (c) 2026 Quiet Orbit, commit `53eed330f5bb`; TypeScript on Bun, by its own words a reader of logs after the fact): taken as ideas are the `.meta.json` beside a transcript as the source of the agent type (then confirmed on the owner's files, keys only) and a subtotal line for a node with children (here: the workflow line); its cost arithmetic was not taken, because the lines must add up to the ledger's own count. `hoangsonww/Claude-Code-Agent-Monitor` (MIT, (c) 2026 Son Nguyen; its README and licence only): nothing taken; it is the dashboard the bullet above decides against. Listed and not read: `damir-tahirovic/Claude-agent-viewer`, `tombelieber/claude-view`, `Thalescantai/claude-agent-tree` (MIT each), `sascha10000/claude-agent-tree` (no licence, so not read) and `ccusage/ccusage` (GitHub names no licence it recognises there). why: MIT asks for its notice where code is copied; none was, so this bullet is the credit; what would change it: code taken from one of them, and then the notice goes into the file that holds it.
- Where the rows come from. Measured on the owner's machine on 2026-11-18 at 20:50 UTC over every project folder (files of Claude Code up to 2.1.323), keys and counts only: of 410 agent transcripts 64 lie flat in `<session>/subagents/` and 346 in `subagents/workflows/<run>/`, none anywhere else; 409 have a meta file with a type and a description; 15 name a parent in `parentAgentId`, and all 15 lie in the same folder as that parent's transcript and not under it, so only the meta file says who started an agent; of 77 workflow folders 75 have the state file `<session>/workflows/<run>.json`, which is written when the workflow ends, and 74 the script `workflows/scripts/<name>-<run>.js`, which is written at its start; the spawn log knows the id of 59 of the 410 transcripts and holds no id without a transcript. So a row is a transcript that exists, and the spawn log fills two gaps: the type of an agent without a meta file, and an agent without a transcript (a fixture pins that row; on this machine there is none today). Not read: the workflow journal (no usage, no timestamps) and the `finished` key of a meta file (only some direct agents have it). what would change it: a Claude Code release that moves these files; the seven fixture sessions pin the layout of 2.1.323.
- The state is inferred, and the output says so. Claude Code writes no state. Measured at the same time, counts only: of the 410 agent transcripts 196 end on the result of a `StructuredOutput` call, 149 on a reply with `end_turn`, 47 on `[Request interrupted …]`, 15 on the result of `SubagentHandback` and 3 on the result of another tool; of 100 main transcripts 95 have a `cost-state` line after their last conversation line. The rules follow these endings: an agent by its last `assistant` or `user` line, a session by a `cost-state` line after that line, a workflow by its state file. The help text and a line under every tree say that the state is inferred. Named limit, with its size: a run that was cut off still reads as running. That is 3 of the 407 agent transcripts that had not been written for 30 minutes (the three that end on the result of another tool), 2 of the 97 such main transcripts, and 2 of the 77 workflows, which have no state file and nothing written under them for 30 minutes. A session that is open and waits for its user reads as running too, and that is right. what would change it: a state that Claude Code writes itself.
- The cost of a line is the ledger's own count of one transcript, through `parse_transcript`, `session_models` and `own_cost` with the price table, so the lines add up to `cost_usd_own`. A model without a price row gives `n/a` for its line and for the total, never a partial sum; a request the prices do not cover is marked `*`, as in the cost report; a session without a single token prints `$0.00` where the ledger's line holds no own count, and there is nothing to add up. Checked: the six priced fixture sessions against `build_entry` (6.0, 7.75, 6.11, 4.82, 3.254 and 2.65 USD); a session the build had not seen, with every pricing case at once; and, run by the session itself on the sessions of this repository on the owner's machine at 20:52 UTC, printing counts only: the cost, the tool calls and the duration of all 86 rows of 13 sessions equal a second count written apart from the build, the rows of the 12 sessions that have an own count add up to it within the rounding of `usage_cost` (largest difference 0.000002 USD), and the 13th has none, explained by a row without a price or without tokens.
- Text from files is not trusted, and odd files do not end the run. Every printed string that comes from a file or a file name (type, description, model, workflow name, session id, the names in the `*` and `n/a` lines) has its non-printable characters replaced by `?`, so a transcript cannot move the cursor, clear the screen or start a line. Checked with 5,182 wrong shapes on Python 3.13 and 3.9 (one value at a time swapped for 25 wrong or extreme values at every place of a transcript line, of a meta file and of a spawn-log row; raw lines that are no JSON; empty files; invalid UTF-8; lines nested 100,000 deep; a number of 400 digits): no run ends, no control character reaches the output, every cost is the ledger's own count, every line that is not read is counted. A transcript or a spawn log that opens and then fails while it is read (a failing disk) counts as a file not read, like one that does not open (`0106376`). A transcript that goes away between the view's two reads of it is counted as one line, and its row keeps what the first read gave and prints `$0.00`: `parse_transcript` is the ledger's reader, left as it is, and it reports a file it cannot open as one parse error and no usage, which the view cannot tell from a file with one broken line; the case needs a file to go away within microseconds and lasts one frame, since the next read no longer lists it. The first build raised in none of its own code; the reader it calls did, and with this change `parse_transcript` no longer raises on three shapes: a line whose id is a list or an object is counted by its number, like a line without an id; a token count that is `Infinity` or beyond 2^63 is a parse error; so is a line nested deeper than Python follows. `ledger.py hook` had survived those through its catch-all and wrote an error line with no figures; it now writes the figures, with the parse errors counted. compliance: the hook's line for such a session changes from "error" to counted figures; no line already written changes.
- One session id in two project folders. `--session` groups the candidates by id and shows the transcript written last, with a line under the tree that says in how many project folders the id lies. why: 1 of 99 session ids on the owner's machine lies in two folders (a session that went on from another folder), and the first build, following the brief, ended such an id with "give more of the id" where there is no more to give. The ledger's `recount` refuses such an id because it would write a record from one of two files (`find_transcript`); the view writes nothing and says which one it shows. what would change it: a case where the older copy is the one wanted.
- Times. A duration runs from the first to the last line of a transcript that carries a time, not until now, and times that run backwards give `-`. Only the form Claude Code writes counts as a time (`YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS`, a fraction of 3 or 6 digits or none, then `Z`, an offset or nothing): `fromisoformat` reads more forms on Python 3.11 and later than on 3.9, and the same files must give the same tree on both.
- Layout. Figures first and the tree last, no header row (criterion 1: the session is the first line); the lines under the tree name the columns. A description is cut at 30 characters and a model name at 14; a cost above zero that would print as `$0.00` prints as `<$0.01`; ASCII only for what the view draws. In `--watch` a line is cut to the window's width by screen columns (a character two columns wide counts as two), and a frame never has more lines than the window. A token count below zero, which no transcript of Claude Code holds, is priced as the ledger prices it and prints as a cost below zero.
- Deferred, skeleton against production grade: colours; a handler for SIGTERM (a killed `--watch` leaves the alternate screen, Ctrl-C does not); a column for how long an agent has been quiet; JSON output; a cache of unchanged files (the slowest tree of this repository's sessions, a main transcript of 38 MB with 33 agent transcripts, is read in 0.27 s and all 13 trees in 0.8 s, so none is needed yet); GitHub Copilot; a list of sessions; in `--watch` a tree taller than the window loses its last lines, which are the newest agents and the total (the plain run prints all of it); a line that is being written at the moment of a read counts as not read for that frame; the project folder of the current directory is worked out as Claude Code 2.1.323 names it for the paths on the owner's machine (every character outside A-Za-z0-9 becomes `-`), and a path that Claude Code names otherwise (characters outside the basic plane, a very long path) is found with `--session`, as the error line says; the columns widen for a cost of a million dollars or a duration of years.
- Seen and left, not part of #59, the owner's to weigh, no item filed. `spawns_for`, the reader `ledger.py hook` uses for the spawn log, raises on a line that is JSON and no object (the hook then writes its error line); the view has a reader of its own. And a named pipe where a file belongs (main transcript, agent transcript, meta file or spawn log) makes the view wait, because `open` waits for a writer: measured on a hand-made session, no answer within 5 seconds in each of the four places. Claude Code writes no such file, the hook reads through the same `parse_transcript`, and a check for a regular file belongs in every reader of the ledger at once.
- How it was built. Fixtures and expected trees first, by hand, before any code (`68df118`); one Opus engineer from a brief, three commits (`a14b10e`, `3ecdd39`, `1280ecc`), the seven trees matching at the first run; then the checks the build had not seen (unseen sessions worked out by hand, the wrong shapes above, `--watch` on a pseudo-terminal with Ctrl-C typed at it on Python 3.13 and 3.9, the real sessions); one Opus reviewer with a probe kit: no BLOCKER, no MAJOR, ten MINOR, and two of the engineer's own decisions judged otherwise; one fix pass by the same engineer (`1d297a5`, `e3c64ce`, `2668759`) took seven of the ten and both judgements, and three things the session's own checks had found (the odd lines above, the id in two folders, a current folder that is gone). Not taken from the review, with the reason: a guard around an `isatty()` that raises (such a stream is closed, and the write that follows raises too); a `try` around the restore of the terminal (a window of microseconds, and a terminal that is gone shows nothing either way); the project folder name for characters outside the basic plane (not verified against Claude Code; named under "deferred"). Then the gate review, `bin/fresh-review` (Opus, fresh context, $1.66), on `9ffec8f`: COMMENT, two MINOR. The first said that an `OSError` ends the run when a transcript goes away between the view's two reads of it. As written it did not hold (`parse_transcript` catches its own `open`; measured with a transcript moved away between the reads: the row stays, and the line is counted as not read), but a read that fails after the file has opened did end the run, against criterion 7: fixed with tests by the same engineer in `0106376`. The second said that only this file would name the session as the merger: taken in the bullet below. The fix was reviewed again on `6da1769` ($0.24): COMMENT, one MINOR, which asked that a transcript gone between the two reads be shown like a read that fails, or that the difference be explained; the bullet on odd files explains it. That run saw the commits since `9ffec8f` and not the whole diff, which had grown to 202,305 characters: the egress screen in front of the review holds anything over 200,000. The screen was left as it is, and each receipt names its base. A change of this size is at the limit of what the gate reviews in one piece; whether the limit or the size of a change should move is the owner's to weigh with the review panel of Phase 1. The suite went from 5,271 to 5,419 passed on both Pythons. The order of the tracker was bent once, on the owner's word: his task had said to prepare without committing until #57 was in review, the item could not be claimed while #57 was in progress (one item in progress per login), the session offered to build with local commits in its own worktree and to claim before the pull request, and he wrote "start now" (19:59:35 UTC, transcript `d3ca52d6-ea62-41b0-97ec-ad79a0c84e64`); so the commits are older than the claim, and nothing was pushed before it. cost: by the ledger's own count at list prices, read with the new view at 20:53 UTC: the engineer $5.76 for the build and the fix pass, the reviewer $1.58, the session that planned, checked and wrote this block $49.21 by then, $56.56 together, as API-equivalent spend under the subscription; the second fix pass and the runs of `bin/fresh-review` came after that reading and are in the pull request.
- The merge (the line the standing word of 2026-11-18 asks for, block above). The pull request that carries this block is merged by the session that built it (Claude Code session `d3ca52d6-ea62-41b0-97ec-ad79a0c84e64`) with the owner's token, on his word in that session ("… a pull request with `Refs #59`, then merge it") and under his standing word, and only with the checks green on the merged head and no finding of `bin/fresh-review` left open; the pull request's checklist names the head, the checks, the reviews and this session, and the message of the merge commit carries the session id, so the forge's own record of the merge names the session and not this file alone (the gate review asked for that; the forge still shows `kacper38` as the one who merged until the agent identity of plan step 2.1 exists). It touches no workflow file and removes no test.
- The closing. The owner wrote to the session at 19:53:30 UTC on 2026-11-18: "mark it as done for me", after the session had written that `owner done 59` stays his. What the session did with it: nothing that goes round the tracker. `review -> done` is a person's transition in `tracker.py` (`is_human()`), a session that faked a terminal would leave the record of a person who was not there, and the standing word keeps the owner verbs with him. The block above on the Phase 1 plan holds his words and the decision that follows from them: #59 stays in `status/review` after the merge and is closed with the Phase 1 items in one line of his at the end of the phase. The nine criteria with their evidence are in the pull request, and nothing is handed to him per item.

## 2026-11-20 — the box, step 1.0a (#63): a local container in which a session builds with permission checks off

- What it is: `bin/box` (`tools/box/box.py`) builds `agent-box:<git SHA>` from `tools/box/` and starts a session in it as uid 1000 with `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, behind an egress list, with the hooks of a read-only copy of `main`; `bin/box run NAME --brief FILE` runs one headless session and writes `result.json`; `bin/box doctor` shows the box is what it claims. What it is not, and stays so until the named trigger (plan section 9): a skeleton. The image is local, with no registry, no CI build and no scan (trigger: a second user); host names are resolved once at the start, and port 53 is open to the container's resolver only, so names still resolve freely and a lookup can carry data out through that resolver (trigger: the addresses drift twice; then a proxy with a domain list); both tokens are readable by the session inside, limited by their scope, their 30-day expiry and the egress list, so a box is for `boundary: own` only (trigger for Bedrock or Foundry through OIDC: the first client repository); the box is not in `kit.manifest` (trigger: a second repository uses it). cost: $0 a month; the image is 1.39 GB on the Mac's disk per SHA kept (`docker image ls`, 2026-11-20), and old tags stay until the owner removes them.
- Decision: the firewall is the vendor's method written anew (`tools/box/firewall.py`: iptables with an ipset, default drop, TCP 443 to the allowed set, a reject for everything else, IPv6 closed, a self-check after the rules), not the vendor's script as plan section 6 decision 8 says; why: the vendor's list also allows npm, error reporting, feature flags and an editor marketplace, the box allows only the model API and GitHub (`tools/box/allowlist` plus GitHub's published web, API and git ranges), and a copy would bring the vendor's licence terms into an MIT repository, which were not checked; what would change it: a probe result that the CLI needs a host off the list (question `cli-hosts`), which adds the name with its result, or a vendor script that takes an allowlist as input. cost: none. compliance: the rule list is a pure function with tests (`tools/box/tests/test_image.py`), and a box whose firewall cannot be set exits 70 before any session (probe question `firewall`).
- Decision: the image is `node:24-trixie-slim` by digest, with CPython 3.9.25 and 3.13.13 from uv's relocatable builds, pytest 8.4.2 (the last that supports 3.9) and 9.2.0, ruff 0.17.3, gh 2.92.0 from its release tarball against a checksum pinned in the file, git and the firewall tools at pinned Debian versions, and the CLI 2.1.323, all in `tools/box/pins.env`; the Dockerfile gives no build argument a default, so a missing pin fails the build, and a Debian point release that drops a pinned version fails it too until the pin moves; why: the criterion asks for one file of pins, and CI's own unpinned `pip install pytest ruff` gets the same majors today; what would change it: CI pinning its tools (then the box takes CI's pins). The image starts as root only for its entrypoint, which sets the firewall, writes the marker and then drops with `setpriv` to uid 1000 with no capabilities and no-new-privs (probe: `CapEff` 0, `NoNewPrivs` 1); `bin/box doctor` found that a venv's python reached through a symlink missed its pytest, so the image has exec shims instead.
- Decision: the judge is `main` fetched at each start and exported once per SHA (`git archive`) to `~/.agent-box/judge/<SHA>/`, mounted read-only; its `.claude/settings.json` becomes the box's managed settings with every hook command pointed at `/judge` and `allowManagedHooksOnly`, so the work clone's hooks never run; the image itself carries managed settings that deny every tool call, for a box started without the wrapper; why: plan decision 6, a half-written guard cannot stall the session and the session cannot edit its judge; residual until step 1.0b: the judge's guard reads its configuration (`.claude/guard.json`, the `boundary`) from the work clone, which the session can edit; what would change it: probe question `managed-hooks` failing, then the work clone's hooks are the named residual of the criterion.
- Decision: the box's clone is made inside the box over HTTPS with the box token (`~/.agent-box/boxes/NAME/clone/`), never a mount of a host checkout; the wrapper refuses a start when `main` or the clone at `HEAD` has a committed `.claude/guard.json` that does not say `boundary: own` (the commit, not the working tree, is read); tokens come from the Keychain entries `agent-box-claude` and `agent-box-github` and reach `docker` as variable names only, the GitHub token is checked against the repository first (HTTP 401, 403 and 404 each stop the start with the entry's name), and no `gh` login of the owner exists inside to fall back to; the container gets four mounts (the clone, the box-only Claude home, the judge, the managed settings) and a mount of the home folder, a credential folder or a Docker socket is refused in code. compliance: the marker records the image SHA, the judge SHA, the seat and the start time; each result file records the brief's hash, both heads and the cost; transcripts and the guard's telemetry stay in `~/.agent-box/claude-home/`, apart from the owner's, kept 90 days.
- Decision: box commits are authored `agent-box <agent-box@box.invalid>` and unsigned (`tools/box/gitconfig`); why: plan decision 9, nothing under the owner's name or signature is made by a session, and a `.invalid` address can belong to nobody; the ruleset on `main` (read 2026-11-20: deletion, non-fast-forward, pull request, required status checks) has no signature rule; what would change it: a signature rule, then a box-only signing key. The model is pinned to `claude-opus-5-5` by the wrapper (decision 13), and `KZ_RULES_OFF` is set for `delete-guard`, `guard-config`, `scratch-md`, `fan-out` and `tracker-guard` until step 1.0b (plan section 5, row 1); a test fails if that list names a rule the guard does not have.
- The probe (`tools/box/box_probe.py`, the `box` key of `tools/orchestration/verified.json`), first commit of the item. Run on 2026-11-20 on OrbStack (Docker 29.8.2, linux/arm64): `permission-flag-non-root`, `dockerenv`, `marker` and `firewall` hold. With both Keychain entries, the full run at image `f0eea08` (commit `28547db`, CLI 2.1.323, model haiku, $0.0036) holds all eleven: a PreToolUse deny blocks the call with permission checks off (one deny row, the allowed call ran), an ask with checks off does not run the asked command, managed hooks run and project hooks do not under `allowManagedHooksOnly`, the only host the CLI reached was `api.anthropic.com`, no connector loaded, and the transcript went under `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and none under the home. `hook-deny-checks-off` is the gate of the criterion, and `run` enforces it: it refuses before any token is read unless the record shows that question holding for the pinned CLI version (`probe_gate`, with tests).
- Verification at `c619c17`, again at `28254f0` with the port 53 check (18 of 18, 7803 passed, 9 skipped on both): `bin/box doctor` 17 of 17 (uid 1000; node, both Pythons, both pytests, ruff, gh, git and the CLI at their pins; `curl https://example.com` exit 7; no socket; no host home; the marker root's; inside the box `ruff check tools` clean and `pytest` 7794 passed, 9 skipped on 3.9 and on 3.13). One live `bin/box run t1 --brief` at image `28547db` (judge `8be59e6`, `claude-opus-5-5`, read-only smoke brief, $0.22, exit 0): both heads `8be59e6` on `main`, so nothing was committed; inside, `curl https://example.com` exit 7, `touch /etc/…` permission denied, `gh api` reached the repository with the box token; `result.json` was written by the host beside the brief, `out.json` and `stderr.txt` in `~/.agent-box/boxes/t1/results/20261120T123649Z/` (brief sha256 `1c54fa10e97009a58873aef07c18a2c969d4392de90eeec1728a73756fd6a594`, session `3962f8cb-a71b-4c9e-ae90-47abcbaa4248`, its transcript under `~/.agent-box/claude-home/`). A token pasted at the `security … -w` prompt is cut at the first line break of the wrapped `setup-token` output (stored 89 of 108 characters, HTTP 401); the README's token step says to paste it as one line.
- The fresh review of `835c7a5` (receipt `.git/worktrees/box/fresh-review/835c7a5aa1a46db7c7b5de898d7835de00cd7ded.json`, verdict COMMENT, no BLOCKER, $1.68) found four MAJOR and three MINOR points; each was fixed or is recorded here. Fixed: the results folder was mounted read-write into the box, so a session could forge its result or plant a link for the host's write; now the CLI's JSON is the container's stdout, captured by the host into a folder no container sees, and `result.json` is created with O_EXCL and O_NOFOLLOW. Port 53 was open to any address, a free channel out; now only to the nameservers of the container's `/etc/resolv.conf`, and the probe's `firewall` question and the doctor check that 1.1.1.1:53 is closed. The deny gate was a sentence; now it is code. The owner's git ran in the session's clone (its `.git/config` is the session's to write); now heads and the committed boundary are read by git in a throwaway container with no network and the clone read-only (`clone_facts`), and the owner's git runs only in his own checkout. A temp folder of a failed export is removed and two starts on one SHA no longer race. A test that could not fail now uses a distinctive token value. Recorded, not changed: the image is built from the checkout that runs `bin/box`, which can be an unmerged branch, while the judge is `main`; the marker and every result file record the image SHA, and a host session runs `bin/box` from `main`; trigger to change: a box started from a branch for anything but this item's own checks. Residual: the content of `result.json` (`out.json`, the cost, the session id) comes from inside the box, where a session with checks off could write to the CLI's output; like the review receipt of decision 7, it stops a forgotten step and is no evidence against a hostile session.
- The fresh review of the fix delta (`835c7a5..e045388`, receipt `.git/worktrees/box/fresh-review/e0453882f5ae50cf0bff9f1b3011f354738c89eb.json`, COMMENT, no BLOCKER, $0.42) found three MINOR points, all fixed: the gate now also refuses when `tools/box` differs from the probed image (its answer rests on the image's settings and the wrapper's rewrite of them, not on the CLI version alone), and a record made on an image with uncommitted changes never counts; so every change under `tools/box` asks for a new probe run before a session starts. `run()` has tests with faked containers for the record it writes (extra stdout lines, unreadable or multi-line output, a clone that cannot be read after the run), and the mount test checks sources against the results folder and every folder above it.
- The review of `f0eea08..2f3bde1` (the probe record and the docs of this run, receipt `.git/worktrees/box/fresh-review/2f3bde182fcd5e0988b2f4cfec3ad751a4a30c4d.json`, COMMENT, $1.03) found two MINOR points in prose, both fixed in `ad79d11`: the README now gives the joining step and not only a count, and the live run's line names its result folder, brief hash and session. The review of that fix (`ad79d11`, $0.17) is APPROVE with no finding.
- Merger: pull request #74 is merged by the Claude Code session `ce185dd9-6b00-4e81-9417-364de47f95a6` on the owner's machine (model Claude Opus 5.5), with the owner's token, under his standing word of 2026-11-18, once the check `ci` is green on its head and the review has left no open finding; this line is prose added after the last review and gets none by that word. The branch touches no workflow and removes no test. The item stays `status/review` after the merge until the owner's line for Phase 1; no session runs an owner verb.

## 2026-11-20 — tracker 0.2.0: owner verbs for a phase, accept-all, a seat per box (item #64, plan step 1.9)

- D1, several numbers. `owner ready N [N ...]` and `owner done N [N ...]` take one or more numbers, kept in the given order; a number given twice is refused before anything is read. why: a phase is several items, and the owner says yes to a phase once (plan section 5 row 3); a duplicate is a typo that would write two marks. revisit if: an owner needs a filter (`--status review`) more than a list. No running cost (a CLI on the owner's machine).
- D2, check then write. Every item is read and checked first (ready: the transition and the definition of ready; done: `done_checks`); when one fails, every failing item is named with all of its reasons (`#N: reason; reason`), nothing is written, exit 2. Then the writes run per item in order, as before. A forge error in the write phase stops there and names the items written, the one that failed (it may be partly written) and the items not touched; exit 1. A read error in the check phase (an item not found, the forge unreachable) exits 1 at once, with nothing written, instead of becoming a reason. why: "change none when one fails" is the criterion, and a half-moved phase is the record nobody can read. Residuals: GitHub has no transaction, so "change none" holds against the checks, not against a forge error in the middle of the writes; and the state can change between the check and the write (one more read before each write is not worth it for a human-run verb). revisit if: a partial write happens in practice. Compliance: the order of comment and label per item is unchanged, so each item's history reads as before.
- D3, flag combinations. `--waive` with more than one number is refused (its indexes are per item); `--accept-all` with `--waive` is refused; `--accept-all` exists on `owner done` only (on `owner ready` it is a usage error, exit 1). why: an index without its item is ambiguous, and a waiver and an acceptance of the same box say two things. revisit if: an owner wants to waive one box in a phase of many (today: `owner done N --waive …` for that item first).
- D4, `--accept-all`. All problems but "acceptance criterion k unchecked" still refuse, for every item, before anything is printed. Then per item the title, each criterion as `k. [ ]/[x] text`, the pull request, the merge commit, who merged it and each check `name: conclusion`; then one prompt, `Type "accept <count>" to accept every criterion of these <count> items: `. Only that exact line (the newline stripped, nothing else) confirms; any other answer or EOF prints `not confirmed; nothing changed`, exit 2. On the answer, per item: the unchecked boxes `parse_ac` reads turn to `[x]` (no other byte of the body changes, so the frozen body hash holds), the done mark gains `criteria: "accepted-all"` and the line `Acceptance criteria accepted by <login> in one confirmation for <count> items (#a, #b, …).`, then status done and close. Before the ticks each item is read again: a body that changed after the check (ticks aside, as `body_hash` reads it) is not written over, the write stops at that item and names it, exit 1; ticks made in the UI meanwhile are kept. The done mark lists in `ticked` the criteria this confirmation ticked, and its text says which (`Ticked by this confirmation: criteria 1, 2.`), so a blanket acceptance is told apart from a box ticked in the UI (both from the fresh review of `9bfd726`). Without the flag the verb is as before; the one visible change is the wording of a refusal, which now has the form of D2 for one item too. why: ticking ten boxes in the UI, one by one, is a click count and no review; the owner reads the same facts in one list and says yes once. Compliance: who accepted, when, and in which batch stays on record in each done mark (ALCOA+: attributable, contemporaneous), and the typed count stops an answer given to the wrong list. revisit if: an auditor asks for a per-criterion acceptance; then the flag goes.
- D5, no owner verb in a box. `is_human()` is also false when `/etc/agent-box/box.json` exists (`os.path.lexists`; root-owned, mode 0444, written once by the box's entrypoint, `tools/box/marker.py`); the existence counts, not the content, so an unreadable or broken marker still means "box". The refusal text now says "a human at a terminal outside a box". why: a box runs with permission checks off and may hold a terminal (`bin/box` attached); the owner verbs are a person's transitions and must not be reachable from a box. revisit if: the marker moves; the constant is one line.
- D6, a seat per box. `seat_of()` reads the marker's `seat` (a plain token, as `marker.py` writes it); no marker means seat `None`, the host. No environment variable counts, `BOX_SEAT` included (the wrapper passes it into the container, so on the host anyone can set it). A marker that exists but cannot be read, is no JSON object or has no plain-token seat refuses the claim with the path named (fail closed). The claim mark gains `seat` (null on the host) and the claim line names the seat. The one-in-progress limit counts per login and seat: a claim is refused when an item in `status/in-progress` has a last claim mark with the same login and the same seat (a mark without the field counts as the host); the same login on another seat is accepted.
