No cap on the number of seats is built (the plan's "three at most" describes the setup, it is no mechanism). why: the host and each box are separate workers under one login, and one item each keeps the rule's point (one branch per worker). Compliance: a seat comes only from a root-owned file, and each claim records it. Residual: on the host, whoever can write `/etc/agent-box/box.json` (root) gets a second seat, and in exchange loses the owner verbs there. revisit if: boxes run under logins of their own; then the seat is the login.
- D7 and D8, version and tests. Version 0.1.0 -> 0.2.0; the usage lines show the new forms. Tests reach the marker only through `BOX_MARKER` patched into `tmp_path` (an autouse fixture in `tools/tracker/tests/conftest.py`), so no test reads or writes `/etc`; this matters inside a box, where the real marker exists. Expected outputs first (`7476701`), then one commit per topic.

## 2026-11-20 — guard 0.10.0: the boundary only tightens, cloud launches are denied, the harness's GitHub tools are read (item #65, plan step 1.7)

- How it was built: held-out hash first (`bdff5d5`, host), the rows before any code (`04f2361`), the probe's two canaries (`ce0b309`) and its record (`e1ebdb6`, host, owner's Mac), then one commit per topic (`be2212a` test isolation, `193f51b` profile, `00582b1` boundary, `614c407` cloud-launch, `c235eb5` guard-config paths, `5e56c4d` shell writes, `cc46ea5` credentials, `5972ae3` refs, `1569abc` approval, `e3153a6` github-tools, `c35c554` matchers and tool kinds, `fe71a6e` version and docs; the box pushed the last three as `257c548`, `69d0def` and `a7590ae`, and the host reworded the subject of the first, 79 characters where the commit gate allows 72, which rewrote all three with an identical tree), built in a box with the design D1–D12 settled by the host session. Compliance angle for every line below: each deny in a cloud session leaves a decision-log line naming its profile, and every changed 0.9.0 verdict is listed in `tools/guard/tests/test_never_weaker.py` with the decision that made it.
- D1, the profile. What: `profile()` says `cloud` when `CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE` is true (the truth test of `disposable_environment`), else `host`; `PROFILES` has one entry per profile (does `rules_off` count, what an ask becomes), so step 1.0b's `box` is one entry; in `run_hook`, where `rules_off` is applied, `host` keeps 0.9.0 exactly and `cloud` ignores `rules_off` from every source and turns the ask into a deny with the prefix `cloud session (CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE): …`; the `host_install` knob is no `rules_off` and still silences `host-install` under `auto`; every log line has `profile`. Nearest choice inside the scope: the table turns the combined ask, after `combine()`, not each ask before it, so a genuine deny keeps its own reasons and a converted ask is not joined to it (the row "a deny is unchanged in a cloud session" asks for this; the effect on every ask is the same). Why: nobody can answer an ask in a cloud session, and a silenced ask there would be a merge or a write with no person behind it. Revisit if: a cloud session gets a channel to the owner, or step 1.0b adds the box profile.
- D2, the boundary. What: a committed value other than `own` or `client` counts as `client`; `KZ_BOUNDARY=client` sets client, `own` changes nothing, any other value (`Client`, `OWN`) counts as client; a refused loosening and an unknown value print a note; `rules_off` needs the committed `own` and an effective `own`; the default without `guard.json` stays `own`. Why: whoever starts a session sets the variable, so it may only add care. Revisit if: the installer writes `boundary: client` by default (step 2.3 of the orchestration plan).
- D3, `cloud-launch`. What: deny `claude` with `--cloud`, `--remote` (or `=value`) or an argument that runs `/autofix-pr`, read through segments (also an unbalanced line) and the reader; `Agent`/`Task` with `isolation` remote (any case; not text: ask); the `schedule` skill (after a `plugin:` prefix and a `/`); a `Workflow` whose `script` or `scriptPath` file matches `isolation: 'remote'` (unreadable: ask); every `RemoteTrigger` call (decided here, not in the plan). `CronCreate`, `ScheduleWakeup` and `loop` stay silent. Host's answer after the first run: `DesignSync` asks through this rule's tool table, because it sends material to a service of the account and the guard cannot read what. Named limits: a `Workflow` by `name` is not resolved (step 1.2); a Workflow script that builds `isolation` at run time is not seen. Why: cloud work runs outside the boundary and outside these hooks. Revisit if: the CLI adds another launch form (the tool-list canary names a new tool).
- D4, matchers and tool kinds. What: the guard's pre and both post matchers in `.claude/settings.json` add `Monitor|ExitWorktree|Skill|Workflow|RemoteTrigger|DesignSync|mcp__github__.*` (the probe's `mcp-hook` holds, so the last part stays); `CLAUDE_TOOLS` gains `Monitor` (`shell`: every shell rule judges its `command`), `ExitWorktree` (`worktree`: `action: remove` asks through `delete-guard`, `keep` is silent), `Skill`, `Workflow`, `RemoteTrigger`, `DesignSync`; an `mcp__github__<op>` tool has kind `github`. `.github/hooks/guard.json` has no matcher, and a Copilot tool named `github-mcp-server-<op>` is read like `mcp__github__<op>`: unverified, fixtures only. Why: a hook that is not called judges nothing. Revisit if: a capture of the work harness shows the Copilot name form, or the probe's `mcp-hook` stops holding.
- D5, `github-tools`. What: the op after the prefix gets the verdict of its twin, built as an event and judged by the existing rules (deny wins), reason `github-tools: <tool> is the same act as <twin>: <reason>`: `get_*`/`list_*`/`search_*`/`*_read` silent, `get_file_contents` a `Read` of its path; branch, pull request, its text, comment reviews silent; merge, auto-merge and an approving review are their `gh` twins (ask); issue and label writes the `gh issue|label` twins `tracker-guard` denies; `create_or_update_file`/`push_files`/`delete_file` a `Write` of each path for `secret-write`, `guard-config`, `scratch-md` (never `protected-branch`), `git rm` for a delete, and a push to the branch (or `main` when it is missing) when that branch is protected; any other op asks; input it cannot read asks. Why: the same act through another door gets the same answer. Revisit if: the GitHub MCP server renames or adds write ops (they ask until named).
- D6, an approval. What: `gh pr review … --approve`/`-a` asks in `tracker-guard`, in the merge's style; `--comment` and `--request-changes` stay silent. Why: an approval counts as a person's; it also gives D5's approve tool a twin. Revisit if: the ruleset stops counting a session's approval.
- D7, `guard-config`, wider. What: also any file below `.github/workflows/`, `tools/testguard/`, `tools/scan/`, `tools/tracker/`, and `tools/commit_msg.py`, matched by path parts as `tools/guard` is (so `docs/tools/scan/x` asks too; the row of the first run that wanted it silent now names `docs/scan/x.md`); shell writes to every guarded path ask (redirections with `>|` and continued lines, `tee`, `cp`/`mv`/`install`/`ln`/`rsync` with `-t`, `dd of=`, `sed -i`, `perl -i`, `truncate`, `touch`, `chmod`/`chown`, `curl -o`, `wget -O`, `patch`/`git apply` by operand or by a diff header in a here-document, `git checkout <ref> -- <path>`, `git restore`, and inline `python -c`, `node -e`, `perl -e`, `ruby -e` code that names such a path and holds a write word), through the reader and both name foldings; reads stay silent. Named limits: a branch switch, merge, pull, rebase or stash that changes these files; a patch whose target is not on the line; obfuscated interpreter code; inline code that names a guarded path only to read it while writing elsewhere asks. Why: the guard and the gates are what keeps a session honest, and a shell was the open door. Revisit if: the false asks of inline code show up in the decision log.
- D8, the stored GitHub login. What: `secret-shell` denies `gh auth token`, `gh auth status -t|--show-token`, `git credential fill|approve`, `git credential-<helper> get` (any helper), `security find-*-password` with `-w`/`-g`, `security dump-keychain`, a shell read of `~/.config/gh/hosts.yml` or `~/.git-credentials` (matched by the path's end, any case), `unset GH_TOKEN|GITHUB_TOKEN`, `env -u` of them, an empty `GH_TOKEN=`, and `env -i` in front of `gh` or `git` (direct or through a shell's `-c`); `secret-read` denies the `Read` tool on the same files. Departures of the first run, accepted by the host: `gh auth token --help` stays silent; the empty assignment is read from the line's text. Why: a session holds its own scoped token; the owner's login reaches every repository. Revisit if: a session needs `gh auth status -t` for a diagnosis (it does not today).
- D9, the refs. What: `git-guard` denies `git update-ref` naming `refs/remotes/` (and `--stdin` when `refs/remotes/` is on the line), every writing form of `git replace`, `git symbolic-ref` writing or deleting under `refs/remotes/` (one operand is a read and stays silent); asks on `git remote set-url|rename|remove|rm`, `git config` writing a remote's `url`, `pushurl` or `fetch` (or dropping or renaming its section), and a fetch into an explicit `refs/remotes/` destination. Named limits: `git fetch . <sha>:<other ref>` and direct writes under `.git/` match nothing. Why: the guard and the tracker read `refs/remotes/` as the remote's state. Revisit if: a rule starts reading the remote's state over the network instead.

The change is prose in this file and in the plan's status; no workflow file and no test is touched.

## 2026-11-21 — guard 0.11.0: a box profile that logs or denies and never asks (item #66, plan step 1.0b)

- How it was built: held-out hash first (`ed62dcd`, host), the rows and their tests before any code (`72e540b`), the scratch-md fixture fix (`091e5b5`), then one commit per topic (`09642a3` facts and predicates, `b6c4059` configuration in a box, `9b30de8` local deletes, `c0201b6` the merge, `356c766` a row correction, `2b3a344` the profile, its table and the logged line, `a1f57fc` the wrapper, `157b60e` version and docs, `dfa28e5` the demonstration), built in a box with the design D1–D10 settled by the host session. Compliance angle for every line below: every act the profile lets run leaves a `logged` line in the decision log with the rule, the call id and the guard's version, so an auditor can list what ran unasked; every deny leaves a line naming profile `box`.
- D1, facts, one seam. What: `BOX_MARKER = "/etc/agent-box/box.json"`, `DOCKERENV = "/.dockerenv"`; `box_facts()` is the only code that touches them and gathers `dockerenv`, `marker_exists`, `marker`, `marker_uid`, `marker_writable`, `marker_dir_writable`, `guard_writable`, `euid`; any exception gives the facts of no marker; called once per hook call through the module global and passed on. Departure: the marker is read with `O_NOFOLLOW`, only when it is a regular file, and a marker over 64 kB counts as no JSON (`marker` None), each a tightening. Why: one seam the tests and the held-out rows replace. Revisit if: the marker's path or schema changes (`tools/box/marker.py`).
- D2, two predicates. What: `in_box` = `/.dockerenv`, a marker, and a guard file and folder this process cannot write; `box_valid` adds a root-owned marker neither it nor its folder writable, a guard not running as root, `schema: agent-box/1`, `boundary: own` in the marker and an effective boundary own. The `guard_writable` fact is no security check: it tells the judge (a read-only mount at `/judge`, the only copy the box's hooks run) from a working copy such as `/work/tools/guard/guard.py`, which the tests and the session run, so the work clone's test suite answers in a box as in CI; a session that makes a copy read-only gains nothing, because its hooks still run the judge. Why: criterion 2. Revisit if: the box's hooks stop running from a read-only judge.
- D3, configuration in a box (any marker, valid or not, since it only tightens). What: `load_config` reads `<guard root>/.claude/guard.json` (three folders above the guard's real path, `/judge` in a box), without its `rules_off` and without `guard.local.json`; the work clone's nearest `guard.json` counts only as a tightening to client; `KZ_RULES_OFF`, `KZ_PROTECTED_BRANCHES`, `KZ_SCRATCH_MD`, `KZ_HOST_INSTALL` are ignored, each with one stderr note; `KZ_BOUNDARY` (tightens only) and `KZ_TELEMETRY_DIR` are read. Without a marker `load_config` is the 0.10.0 code (only `KZ_BOUNDARY`'s reading moved into `_env_boundary`, unchanged). Departures: the work clone's `guard.json` counts as client for any `boundary` other than `own` (an unknown value tightens, as 0.10.0's D2), not only for `client`; `load_config` takes the facts as a second argument and reads them itself when none are handed in. Named residual: on the host `KZ_PROTECTED_BRANCHES` and `KZ_SCRATCH_MD` can still loosen (PR #76, D12 (e)); the box closes it only for the box. Why: a session must not configure its own judge. Revisit if: the host gets a judge copy of its own.
- D4, the profile. What: `profile(environ, facts, cfg)`: `cloud` when `CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE` is true (it wins), else `box` when `box_valid`, else `host`; `PROFILES["box"] = {"rules_off": False, "ask": None, "table": BOX_TABLE}`; `run_hook`, `run_post` and `log_decision` pass the facts they have. Departure: a call with no facts (the one-argument call of 0.10.0) reads `box_facts()` and loads the configuration of the process's cwd; every caller in the guard passes both, so this path serves only outside callers. A box with an invalid marker runs `host` with D3's configuration, so its asks stay asks. Criterion 7: the probe of step 1.0a showed that an ask does not pass unanswered with permission checks off (`tools/orchestration/verified.json`, `box.questions.hook-ask-checks-off`: holds), so its condition is not met and nothing changed for it. Revisit if: that probe answer changes in a later CLI.
- D5, the box table. What: one entry per ask class (the rules of `RULES` and `unreadable-args`, `budget`): `log` for `delete-guard` (local deletes only), `guard-config`, `scratch-md`, `fan-out`; `merge` for `tracker-guard`; `deny` for every other; a missing class is a deny (`BOX_TABLE.get(name, "deny")`) and a test fails when the table's keys differ from the classes. A rule's deny is untouched; an ask a rule gives because it could not read the call (`_is_reader_reason`, built from `READER_MSG`, `_PIPE_NOT_READ`, the reader's stop messages and the crash text, with a test over each) is a deny whatever the class; any other ask is turned by its entry, a deny reason prefixed `box session: no one answers an ask here, so it is denied.` A call with a deny answers deny and logs each rule's verdict after the table; a call with only logged rules answers nothing (exit 0) and logs one `logged` line; no `skipped` lines in a box. Departure: each rule's ask is turned in the rules' child process (`_rule_verdict`), not in the parent after it, so the checks of D6 and D7 (the latter runs `git`) stay inside the 3.5 s budget; a rule that does not finish in time is the `budget` class, a deny. Why: no one answers an ask in a box, and a silenced ask would be an act with no record. Revisit if: a class turns out to need a person (it moves to `deny`).
- D6, local deletes. What: `_box_local_delete(ev)` re-reads the call with the rule's own expressions and readers (text, folded names, here-documents, each script the call names, every simple command) and answers True only when no form reaches past the box: the `rm` class, `find -delete`, git's local verbs, `update-ref -d refs/heads/…`, the code forms and `ExitWorktree` remove are local; any push that deletes or forces, `update-ref` outside `refs/heads/`, every tool of `TOOL_RE`/`_DELETE_TOOLS`, SQL, a shell fed by a pipe and any error are not. Departure: a script the call names that cannot be opened counts as holding nothing, as the rule itself scanned nothing there (the ask then comes from the line's own text, which is checked). Why: deleting files in the box's own clone is the work; the remote and the host's services are not the box's. Revisit if: the replay shows a local form denied.
- D7, the merge. What: a `tracker-guard` ask is logged only for one simple command `gh pr merge N --match-head-commit SHA` (or `=SHA`), with at most one of `--merge`, `--squash`, `--rebase`, nothing else, a full 40-character lower-case hash, and the newest `fresh-review` receipt for that hash under the git dir of the call's folder (`git rev-parse --git-dir`, 3 s) a JSON object with that `sha` and verdict `APPROVE` or `COMMENT`; else a deny naming the first condition that failed. The harness's merge tools (`mcp__github__merge_pull_request`, auto-merge) carry no head hash this check reads: denied by `github-tools`'s entry. Departure: a receipt over 1 MB counts as no receipt. Named limits: the receipt is a file in the box's own clone, which a session could write itself, so the check stops a forgotten review, not a hostile session (plan section 6, decision 7); GitHub enforces `--match-head-commit`, the guard does not compare the pull request's head itself. Revisit if: receipts are signed or written outside the box.
- D8, never weaker. What: `baseline-0.10.0.jsonl`, written by 0.10.0 (`6a1e3f6`) over the corpus of `test_never_weaker.py` plus the text tables of 0.10.0 (`SHELL_ROWS`, `TOOL_ROWS`; the docstring of `test_box_profile.py` says what is not covered and why); without a marker (injected, and the real `box_facts` here) every input answers exactly as 0.10.0; with a valid marker no deny changes and no ask remains; the table test; rows for every kind of plan section 4, the merge rows on scratch repositories with receipts. The 0.9.0 test is unchanged. Departure: `356c766`, after the rows, corrects one row: with a valid marker an input whose boundary is client gets no box profile (criterion 2), so it answers exactly as 0.10.0 and keeps its asks; the run's guard root carries the corpus's `host_install: ask`, since a box ignores `KZ_HOST_INSTALL`. Why: criterion 8. Revisit if: a later version changes a deny (it gets its own listed change).
- D9, the wrapper. What: `tools/box/box.py` has no `RULES_OFF` and `docker_argv` sets no `KZ_RULES_OFF`; `test_rules_off_names_only_rules_the_guard_has` keeps its name and checks both; the only mention left under `tools/box/` and `bin/` is the docstring that says it is gone. Plan section 5, row 1 ends here. Why: the profile replaces the silencing. Revisit if: never (a silencing comes back only through a new plan row).
- D10, version and docs. What: `VERSION = "0.11.0"`; `README.md` and `docs/SYSTEM.md` name the profile, its table, the merge form and receipt, the configuration beside the guard and the end of `KZ_RULES_OFF`. Named limit: the decision log lives in `/box-home/telemetry`, a folder the session can write, so its lines record and do not prove; the host's copy of the log and the pushed history are the evidence. Revisit if: the log moves to a folder the session cannot write.
- Demonstration (criterion 9), `tools/guard/tests/evidence-0.11.0-box.txt`: re-recorded in this box at `2864f83` (marker `root:root 0444`, marker judge `6a1e3f6e813d`, `/.dockerenv`, uid 1000, `KZ_RULES_OFF` still set by this run's wrapper and ignored), seven PreToolUse events through a read-only `mktemp -d` copy of the guard whose sha256 `192ea9c5fa4c` equals `HEAD:tools/guard/guard.py` `192ea9c5fa4c`, in a scratch repository cloned from `/work` that holds the judge's commit, head A adding `docs/demo-a.md` and head B changing `tools/guard/guard.py`, both from the judge's commit, nothing run: `rm -rf build` silent, exit 0, line `delete-guard: logged`; `git push origin --delete x` deny, exit 2, `delete-guard: deny`; `cat .env` deny, exit 2, `secret-shell: deny`; `gh pr merge 76 --merge --match-head-commit <A>` with no receipt deny, exit 2, `tracker-guard: deny`, reason "no fresh-review receipt exists"; the same after a COMMENT receipt for A silent, exit 0, `tracker-guard: logged`; `gh pr merge 77 --merge --match-head-commit <B>` with a COMMENT receipt for B deny, exit 2, `tracker-guard: deny`, reason names `tools/guard/guard.py`; the same after `git replace <B> <A>` (plain `git diff --name-only <judge> <B>` then shows only `docs/demo-a.md`) still deny, exit 2, `tracker-guard: deny`, reason names `tools/guard/guard.py`. Every line has `guard: 0.11.0`, `profile: box` and the call id. Replaces the record at `814a2e0`, which ran in a fresh repository without the judge's commit (review finding: no end-to-end record of the merge check against the real marker; the unit rows replace `box_facts`).
- Hook cost (one `hook pre` through the real entry point, read-only copies in this box, Python 3.13, median of five): `ls -la` 87.4 ms at 0.10.0, 87.0 ms at 0.11.0; the merge row 89.2 ms (ask) and 92.6 ms (logged, the receipt check's `git rev-parse` included). The budget test passes.

Held-out rows X19 and X20 had hashed a logged merge of a hash that is no commit in their repository; the file keeps them as written and their test names the change. A network lookup of the base was not taken: hooks never call the network (`AGENTS.md`, harness notes). A head built on a newer main than the judge is measured against the judge and may be denied for changes it did not make; that fails closed, and the owner merges it. Minors: the test-file check now also sees `test/` folders, `*_test.py` and `conftest.py`; a row covers a shell suffix run by another interpreter; named limit: a non-shell script whose `git` and `push` are more than 80 characters apart, or whose command is assembled at run time, is not seen by the script check, which reads text; held-out X19 and X20 lose their own coverage of a logged merge, which PATH_ROWS `docs` and `edit-a-test` keep.

The object store is not re-hashed: named limit, and this is where the hardening stops on purpose. The box merge check stops a careless merge of a change to the guard, CI or the tests, not a hostile session: such a session can forge objects, and it holds a token that merges through GitHub's API from Python with no shell form the guard reads (plan section 12: inside a box the guard is the only gate and it matches command text). Against a hostile session the controls are the token's scope (this repository, 30 days) and Phase 2's ruleset with its required review. ## 2026-11-21 — the task page (#77): a local, read-only HTML record of a task, planned by an Opus session

- What: plan `docs/PLAN-2026-11-dashboard.md`, approved by the owner on 2026-11-21 ("i agree to 77") with the four recommended answers of its section 6. One command writes one static HTML file from files already on the machine: tasks grouped by branch, sessions of the host and of the box, agents, every tool call joined with the guard's verdict on the call id, review findings and their re-tests, evidence paths, cost.
- Why a static file and not a console: a dashboard is attack surface (916 internet-exposed consoles of the ARTEX pentest tool on its ports 8787 and 8788 and trojanized images of it were reported in November 2026: SOCRadar, via https://malware.news/t/artex-916-exposed-ai-pentest-consoles-mapped-in-the-u-s/127242, read 2026-11-20), and transcripts may hold client data. So: no server, no port, no network call, no JavaScript, the CSP `default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'` (inline styles accepted because the page has no stylesheet file and `check_page` refuses `url(`, `url=`, `@import` and `://` in `<style>` and `style=`), every text escaped, the file 0600 outside any git work tree, never committed or published. Boundary: titles, folders, branch names and finding summaries stay on the page and can name a client; the page covers `boundary: client` repositories too, because it holds a subset of the transcripts beside it on the same machine, and it never leaves the machine (no ticket, pull request, chat or evidence pack). A branch name from a transcript is checked by `git check-ref-format --branch NAME` (the name as its single argument; that verb takes no `--end-of-options`, exit 129 on git 2.55.0) and reaches `git log` only after `--end-of-options`, when it holds no `@{` and came back unchanged. Cost: about $0 a month; a few USD of tokens to build. Compliance angle: the page shows the audit trail (guard log, review receipts, evidence paths); it is no evidence by itself, the files it reads are. Revisit if: live state is needed beyond what a terminal multiplexer gives (then a Claude Code panel bound to localhost), or a QA reviewer wants the page as a record (then it goes through the evidence export with its sha256).
- Why no tool-call content: commands, paths and outputs can carry client data or secrets; the timeline shows the tool name, the verdict and the time. Revisit if: an open-mode repository needs the content (then an opt-in with redaction, never in `boundary: client`).
- Deferred, named: live refresh, tracker titles from the forge (the branch and PR number name the task, no network), tool-call content, Copilot sessions (Copilot leaves the guard log only), an exploration graph, a re-test button.
- Model routing exception: the plan was written by the Opus session, not by `platform-planner` on Fable, because the Fable limit was reached on 2026-11-20 (reset 2026-11-24) and the owner chose not to wait ("moze zrob plan samemu, nie ma co czekac"). Cost: about $1 of tokens. Revisit if: the owner wants a Fable review of the plan once the limit resets.
- Related, the owner's tool: herdr (herdr.dev, Apache-2.0, a local server `herdr-core`, SSH to other machines, a phone app) was considered as the live view and is complementary, not a replacement: it shows which agent needs attention now and shows no tool calls, guard verdicts, findings or cost. The owner installs it himself (an agent installs nothing). A box tab runs `bin/box run NAME`; its phone link stays off for box tabs, since a session in the box runs with permission checks off; no use in `boundary: client` repositories until a line here says so. Cost: $0. Revisit if: herdr is wanted on client work, or it stops showing agent state through `docker run -it`.
- The fresh review at `08f51de` (COMMENT, 1 MAJOR, 4 MINOR, $0.22), fixed in the next commit: the plan's "no `http`" check could not pass beside the CSP tag's `http-equiv`, so steps 2 and 5 now test for `<script`, `src=`/`href=` other than `#` anchors, `url(` and `@import`; branch names from transcripts reach git only after `--end-of-options` and `git check-ref-format --branch`, with tests; the boundary of the page is named (above and in the plan's section 3); the plan's status names the promotion of #77; the ARTEX figure has its source and read date.
- The fresh review of the delta `--base 08f51de` at `d47f758` (COMMENT, 3 MINOR, $0.23), fixed in the next commit: the external-load check is now `check_page`, an allowlist over the page parsed with `html.parser` (tags and attributes the renderer emits, `href` only as `#`, `meta` only as charset and the CSP tag, no `://`, `url(`, `url=` or `@import` in attribute values or `<style>`), shared by the tests and the live run, so escaped text never counts and meta refresh, `action=` and `srcset=` are caught; a branch holding `@{` or changed by `check-ref-format` gets no evidence lookup.
- The fresh review of the delta `--base d47f758` at `6b8ea06` (COMMENT, 1 MAJOR, 2 MINOR, $0.23), fixed in the next commit: `check_page` requires the CSP tag exactly once with exactly the planned content (missing, doubled or `default-src *` are breaches, each with a test); its allowlist is a fixed list of its own, never derived from the renderer; the `check-ref-format` call takes the name as its single argument, as probed on git 2.55.0 (`--output=x` refused, `--end-of-options` exit 129, `@{-1}` expanded to `main`), and the tests run against the git on the machine.
- The fresh review of the delta `--base 6b8ea06` at `0bc542b` (COMMENT, 3 MINOR, $0.23), answered in the next commit, which is prose only: the full CSP and why inline styles are accepted (above); the other two are written into the plan as requirements of steps 1 and 2 (`refs/heads/NAME` and `--` for `git log`, a branch absent from the clone shown as such, git tests that assert outcomes, build their own reflog and fail when git is missing). With this line every finding of the four reviews is fixed or carried into a named step.
- Merger, under the owner's standing word of 2026-11-18 and his "you can merge when its green" of 2026-11-21: Claude Code session `e7803278-c604-491c-9e5a-00628a1fbc58` on the owner's Mac merges the pull request of this block with the owner's token, after the checks are green on its head, with `gh pr merge --match-head-commit` on that head and the fresh-review receipts of this block, whose findings are all fixed or carried into a named step; the last commit is prose. Docs only: no workflow file is touched and no test is removed. The owner promoted #77 to `status/ready` himself on 2026-11-21.

## 2026-11-21 — the task page (#77) built: `tools/telemetry/page.py`, steps 1–5 of its plan

- Step 1, which sessions a page holds: those of the repository the page is for (`project`): a host session belongs to it when a `cwd` its transcript names is that folder or lies under it (worktrees under `.claude/worktrees/` do; scratch worktrees elsewhere, such as a session's scratchpad, do not), found through the Claude Code project folders whose name starts with the project's encoded name and confirmed by the `cwd`, so a sibling folder of the same prefix (`repo-2`) stays out. A box session is one whose `cwd` is `/work`, where every box mounts its clone; `collect()` reads the homes it is given, and the CLI (step 3) decides which. Cost: $0. Revisit if: sessions of one repository regularly run from folders outside it.
- Step 1, a session's task: the branch most of its lines carry (a tie goes to the branch seen last); every branch and PR number it saw stays on its row. Named limit: a session whose folder is the main checkout and that works in a worktree by absolute paths is grouped under the main checkout's branch, since its lines carry that branch (the session that built this page is one); its PR numbers from `pr-link` lines still name the task. Revisit if: Claude Code records the branch of the worktree a call touched, or the tracker's claim is read (forge data stays out of this version, answer 2).
- Step 1, how a review is tied to a task: a receipt holds no branch, so it belongs to a task when its `sha` is a value `refs/heads/NAME` has had (its reflog, `git log -g`) or a commit of `origin/main..refs/heads/NAME`; the reflog keeps the tie after a merge or a rebase, where `origin/main..NAME` alone is empty once the branch is in main. Receipts are read from the repository's git common dir (`fresh-review/` and every `worktrees/NAME/fresh-review/`), not only from the git dir of a session's folder, because a review run in a worktree lands in that worktree's git dir. A receipt tied to no branch on the page is counted under the reviews, not shown. Limits: a branch deleted, or a reflog older than git's expiry (90 days by default), loses the tie; a branch made from another's tip also shows the other's review at that tip. Revisit if: `fresh-review` writes the branch into its receipt.
- Step 1, git calls: `origin/main` is checked once per page (`git rev-parse --verify --quiet --end-of-options refs/remotes/origin/main^{commit}`), because `git log origin/main..refs/heads/NAME` exits 128 both for a branch absent from the clone and for a clone without `origin/main`; without it every branch says "origin/main not in this clone" instead of blaming the branch. Git runs only in the project folder, never in a box's clone (the box session controls that clone's `.git/config`, which is why `tools/box/box.py` reads it in a throwaway container). On git 2.55.0 every name `check-ref-format --branch` changes also holds `@{` (`@{-2}` comes back as `main`), so with the real git the two checks of section 3 hide each other; a stub that answers `check-ref-format` tests each alone, beside the tests against the real git. Compliance angle: the evidence lookup is read-only and local, and each refusal is shown on the page rather than dropped.
- Step 2, `check_page` holds more than the plan's list, each with its own test: a backslash in `<style>` text or in an attribute value is a breach (a CSS escape such as `\75 rl(` spells `url(` past a text search); a comment, a processing instruction and any declaration but `<!DOCTYPE html>` are breaches (the renderer writes none); the CSP tag must sit inside `<head>` (a browser ignores it elsewhere); every `href` is checked, not only the last of two (a browser takes the first, `dict()` the last); a `<meta>` is the charset or the CSP tag by its full attribute list, so a doubled attribute is a breach. The page has no inline `style=` and no `<div>` outside `.wide` wrappers around tables, so the allowlist has neither `style` nor any event attribute. Planted breaks: 18 of 18 caught (one more, `convert_charrefs=False`, changes nothing, since `html.parser` unescapes attribute values either way and never `<style>` text, so the argument was dropped). Revisit if: the page needs an element or attribute off the list (then the list in `check_page` grows by hand, with a test).
- Step 3, the command `page.py [--days 7] [--session ID] [--out PATH]`: the page is for the main work tree of the repository the current folder lies in (`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`), so a run from a worktree shows the whole repository; the box home is read only when that repository has `bin/box`, since a box clones only the repository that starts it, `tools/box` is not in `kit.manifest`, and a box session holds no repository name, only `/work` (a task repository's page shows no box sessions, and says so in its header). The refusal "inside a git work tree" covers the default place as well as `--out` (`KZ_TELEMETRY_DIR` can point into a repository, and some owners keep `~/.claude` under git), and it walks up the folders for a `.git` entry instead of asking git: stricter than git's discovery, with no git, no config and no `safe.directory` or ceiling setting that could turn it off. A new file only (`O_CREAT | O_EXCL`, so an existing file or a link in its place stays as it is), mode 0600 whatever the umask, the default folder set to 0700 at every run; the page passes `check_page` before it is written; stdout holds the path alone, so a shell can open it. `--session` takes a full id or a prefix that fits one session, and the task's figures then count that session only. Git variables that point at another repository (`GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE` and the like, set inside a git hook) are dropped for every git call. Planted breaks: 15 of 15 caught. Cost: $0. Revisit if: a box session starts to record the repository it cloned (then its sessions can be matched without `bin/box`).
- Step 4, into the kit: one `managed` line in `kit.manifest` (`tools/telemetry/page.py`, beside the ledger it loads by path), rows in `docs/SYSTEM.md` for the script and for its pages with their data class, a README entry. `install.sh` needed no change, though the plan's step 4 names it: `--into` copies every manifest entry, and the home install copies agents and skills only; shown by an install into a scratch repository, where the copy is identical and `page.py --out` writes an empty page that says the box home was not read. Revisit if: the home install starts to ship tools.
